## Editions milestone 1 (22:26 UTC, 0803b5ad)
- Research cited (Miss Blanche: Ishimaru, 56 made, Phillips 2022 $516,600; ACRIFIX must not enter closed cavities;
  anneal 80 C at 1 h/mm; critical angle 42.2 deg; specimens must be BOUGHT not collected; copper inhibits MMA cure; iron
  meteorites rust from chloride). Six editions: Stamper (a Session 00 nickel stamper shard in Bedrock), Offcuts (the
  first eight speakers' tints as strata in Core Sample), Firn (lens voids at every bond line of Moho, Ice), Groove
  (Session 00's waveform as a spiral air channel in Moho), Contour (Shasta's 2,000 m line as an air channel in Relief),
  Iron (an etched Gibeon slice at the center of Core). All clear cast with 3 mm tinted strata. Finals: Firn, Iron, Stamper.

  luminance = cabinet's, as a multiple of stock 1/2 in absorbance), then 0.5-scale 96-sample study frames.

- 03:10 OWNER: "I’m also not loving the footing on the speaker. Why is it that we need it? Is there a way to make it less top heavy,
  or perhaps a way to tilt it back without such a wide footprint? Or a separate base that people can optionally purchase?" Saved

  of 20 % of the weight (max 250 N) at the top: geometric, so the center of mass must sit 0.2 x the push height (261-267 mm) inside each
  tipping edge; on its own feet Strata fails -24 % forward, -34 % back, -12 % sideways; ballast doesn't help. My calc under the other
  reading (13 % / 100 N, which the lane's earlier check used): passes on its own feet (fwd +16 %, back +2 %, side +36 %), +10-20 kg low
  ballast gives +11-24 % at the back; tilting back hurts the back margin unless a rear foot steps ~100 mm out. Research workflow
  wf_a73d7898-d30 running to settle which test applies (with a skeptic checking sources).

- 04:29 stability research done (wf_a73d7898-d30; stability-standard-2026-11-03/). Under IEC 62368-1:2018/2023 a plain MS3
  floor-standing speaker gets 8.6.2.2 (10° tilt, or the 10° incline, or 13% + 50% down at 1.5 m), 8.6.2.3 (800 N) and 8.6.3
  (10° tilt). The 20%/250 N push lives only in 8.6.5 (front controls or display, or a maker's stand under 8.10), and a 15° tilt
  stands in for it. The team's reading mixed IEC 60950-1's 2 m with the 2014 8.6.2.2 force. On its own feet Tropos tips at about
  17.3° back / 20° forward / 22.8° side (com.json strata), so the base isn't needed for safety; a lab's written pre-assessment
  comes before tooling.

  A's outside: satin or polished"): pressure-formed over a male plug, so the outside is the free side and leaves the press with the
  cast sheet's own gloss; polished is no extra work, satin is (a bead blast, ~$15-30 a horn). Gloss's cost: a higher reject rate
  (dust specks and corner ripple show on gloss, hide under satin), so the press needs the clean room the risk register already asks
  for; touch-up by hand buffing ~$10-20 a horn ($30-60 a full buff), never flame on the formed face (crazing). In use: gloss shows
  fingerprints and swirls (polish out); satin hides prints but a scratch can't be buffed out. Satin was the owner's look, not a cover
  for defects. Tooling unchanged ($6,925 / $14,465). The polished-A pair frame (gate2/A-ice-polished.png) after the six now

- 11:41 check-in #39. Study wf_6f2b81e0-333: acoustics and materials lines back, the light line and the skeptic running.

  with the bottom-batt window (204 Hz Q ~28). Materials: fibre and voile can't pass light and damp at once; a rigid clear
  micro-perforated 2 mm acrylic (0.3-0.4 mm holes, 1-1.5 %) over a sealed gap could match the batts 160-630 Hz and pass ~90 % of
  the light (~$70-300 a speaker est., coupon-test first); Barrisol's Microsorber foil is weak at 200 Hz. Either way the openings
  show the inside (HF lead, tie rod, amp compartment): a design call for the owner.
